RETAIL MARKET AT A GLANCE 
Large and small nuts increased by Rs.5.00/nut and Rs.8.00/
nut respectively. Large nuts now range Rs.210.00 -220.00/nut 
while small nuts cost Rs.180.00 -195.00/nut. Compared to last 
year, prices have surged significantly for both large nuts 
(91%) and small nuts (97%). 
                                                                                
                                                                                                
                 (Page No.05) 
Imported dried chilies have increased by Rs.24.00/kg, now 
ranging Rs.700.00 -950.00/kg. Vedalan prices rose by 
Rs.50.00/kg and imported red onion by Rs.2.00/kg.                
Meanwhile, potato prices decreased, with Welimada variety 
decreased by Rs.4.00/kg and Nuwara Eliya variety decreased 
by Rs.2.00/kg. 
                                                 (Page No.02-03) 
Most vegetable prices decreased following reduced demand 
after the festival season. Among up -country varieties, beans 
saw the highest drop at Rs.141.00/kg, while carrots fell by 
Rs.98.00/kg. For low -country vegetables, brinjal prices           
decreased significantly by Rs.312.00/kg, capsicum by 
Rs.223.00/kg, and tomatoes by Rs.99.00/kg compared to last 
week. 
                                                                                             
                                                         (Page No.03-04) 
Most rice varieties have decreased in price, except for             
imported nadu, imported raw white, and local raw white. 
Local raw red saw the largest decrease (Rs.2.00/kg), while 
local raw white had the largest increase (Rs.3.00/kg).              
Compared to last year, local raw red prices are up 19%.  
Farmgate paddy prices remain stable compared to last week, 
with short grain ranging Rs.130.00 -137.00/kg, keeri samba at 
Rs.138.00-147.00/kg in major producing areas, and long 
grain white and red varieties at Rs.124.00 -132.00/kg and 
Rs.120.00-135.00/kg respectively.

Wholesale 
Prices of keeri samba has decreased by 4% 
due to high supply of stocks at the market  
and price ra nged R s.260.00- 280.00/kg. 
Price of raw red and raw white have 
decreased by 2% and 3%  respectively and 
average prices were Rs.213.00/kg and 
Rs.212.00/kg. Price of all other local rice 
varieties have not increased significantly.  
Imported ponni samba has dec reased by 
2% and price ranged Rs.225.00 -235.00. 
Imported nadu and imported raw white 
were also available at the market and      
prices ranged  Rs.215.00-220.00/kg and  
Rs.205.00-210.00/kg. 
Compared to same period of last year, most 
of the rice varieties have  seen price 
increases with the highest price of 22% for 
raw red. Price of keeri samba and imported 
ponni samba have decreased by 15% and 
8% respectively. 
 
Retail 
The retail prices of all types of rice varieties 
except for, imported nadu and imported and 
local white have decreased. Among those a 
highest price decreased of Rs.2.00/kg was 
observed for local raw red and a highest 
price increased of Rs.3.00/kg was observed 
for local raw white.  
 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the highest  price increase of 19% was 
recorded for local raw red and highest price 
decreased of 21% was recorded for keeri 
samba. 
 
Paddy 
Compared to the same period of last week, 
the average farmgate prices of major paddy 
varieties have not changed significantly. 
The average price of short grain paddy has 
ranged between Rs.130.00 -137.00/kg. It 
was reported price range of Rs.138.00 -
147.00/kg for keeri samba paddy in major 
producing areas. Subsequently, the price 
for long grain white and red paddy varieties 
was between Rs.124.00-132/kg and 
Rs.120.00-135.00/kg respectively. 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the average prices of all the major paddy 
varieties except keeri samba have 
increased by 4% -32% and the highest for 
long grain red paddy. Meanwhile the 
average farmgate price of keeri samba 
paddy has decreased by 3%-9%.

Dried Chilies 
Wholesale 
The price of imported dried chilies has 
decreased by 3%   and price ranged 
Rs.500.00-600.00/kg, 
Compared to same period of last year, 
current whole sale price of imported dried 
chilies has decreased by 18%. 
 
Retail 
The retail price of imported dried chilies has 
increased by Rs.24.00/kg and price ranged 
Rs.700.00-950.00 /kg. 
Compared to the same perio d of last year, 
the current retail price of imported dried 
chilies has decreased by 24%. 
 
Onion 
Wholesale 
Price of vedalan red onion   has increased 
by 31%   due to unavailability of sufficient 
stocks and price ranged Rs.330.00 -
350.00/kg. Price of imported red onion has 
increased by 3% and price ranged 
Rs.250.00-350.00/kg. Price of imported 
big onion has decreased by 17% due to 
availability of sufficient stocks and price 
ranged Rs.90.00-100.00/kg. 
Compared to same period of last year, 
current wholesale pric e of vedalan and 
imported red onion have decreased by 
8%and 13% and also imported big onion 
has decreased by 67%. 
 
Retail 
The retail price of vedalan and imported red 
onion have increased by Rs.50.00/kg and 
Rs.2.00/kg respectively. The retail price of 
imported big onion has decreased by 
Rs.2.00/kg. 
 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the current retail prices of all types of onion 
have decreased and among those, the highest 
price decreased of 79% was recorded for 
imported big onion. 
 
Potato 
Wholesale 
The price of Welimada potato and Nuwara 
Eliya potato   varieties have decreased by 
6% and 3% due to availability of sufficient 
stocks and price ranged Rs.250.00 -
260.00/kg and Rs.280.00-290.00/kg. Price 
of imported potato has increased by 6% 

Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin  
 
3  
  
 
due t o availability of high-quality stocks 
and price ranged Rs.150.00-190.00/kg. 
Compared to same period of last year, 
current wholesale price of all potato 
varieties have increased in the range of 4%-
7%. 
 
Retail 
The retail price of Welimada and Nuwara 
Eliya potatoes have decreased by Rs.4.00/kg 
and Rs.2.00/kg respectively. The retail price 
of imported potato has increased by 
Rs.15.00/kg. 
 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the retail price of Nuwara Eliya potato has 
increased by 6% while the retail price of 
imported potato has decreased by 1%.

Pulses 
Wholesale 
Price of red cowpea   has increased by 7% 
due to unavailability of sufficient stocks 
and price ranged Rs.550.00 -700.00/kg. 
Price of white cowpea has decreased by 3% 
and price ranged Rs.650.00 -700.00/kg. 
Prices of green gram and   red dhal have 
not changed significantly and price ranged 
Rs.700.00-800.00/kg   and Rs.250.00 -
260.00/kg. 
Compared t o same period of last year,  
current wholesale prices of all pulses 
varieties have decreased in the range of 
3%-41% respectively. 
 
Retail 
The retail prices of green gram and white 
cowpea have decreased of Rs.49.00/kg and 
Rs.9.00/kg respectively. The retail price of 
red cowpea has increased by Rs.7.00/kg. 
 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the current retail prices of all types of pulses 
have decreased. Among those, a notable 
price decreased of 42% was recorded for 
green gram.  
 
Vegetable 
Wholesale 
Prices of most of the vegetables have 
decreased due increased supply and 
decreased demand during the end of the 
festival season. 
Most of the upcountry vegetable prices are 
decreased, specifically, carrot reported the 

Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin  
 
4  
  
 
highest as 23%, following prices of other 
vegetables except beetroot have decreased 
in the range of 4%-18%. However, price of 
beetroot has increased by 10% with 
availability of stocks from Nuwara  Eliya 
area. Further, with decreased demand price 
of tomato and capsicum have  has 
decreased by 16% and 17% respectively. 
Considering low-country vegetables, most 
of the vegetable varieties experienced price 
decrease. Particularly price of brinjal has 
decreased by 46% while price of ash 
plantain has decreased by 24% with 
decreased d emand. Prices of cucumber, 
snake gourd and pumpkin have decreased 
in the range of 13% - 20%. Prices of luffa, 
bitter gourd and ladies fingers have 
decreased in the range of 7% - 11%. 
However, price of long beans has increased 
by 23% with decreased supply from major 
producing areas while price of drumstick 
has increased by 2%. In addition, price of 
green chilies and lime have decreased by 
54% and 35% respectively. 
Compared to the previous year, most 
vegetable prices have increased, with 
tomato reported the highest as 891%. 
 
Retail 
Retail prices of most vegetable varieties 
decreased in line with the downward 
wholesale trend. In up-country vegetables, 
the highest price decrease was reported for 
beans at Rs. 141.00/kg. Further, the price 
of carrots decreased by Rs. 98.00 /kg.  
 
Considering the low -country vegetable 
varieties, brinjal prices showed a 
significant price decrease of arou nd 
Rs.312.00/Kg. Capsicum prices showed a 
significant price decrease of Rs.223.00 /kg. 
Tomato prices decreased by Rs. 99.00/kg 
compared to the previous week. Lime 
prices decreased by Rs. 30.00/kg.  
However, compared to the same period last 
year, prices of most varieties have 
increased, while a notable price increment 
of 294% was reported for tomato. 
 
 

Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin

Coconut 
Wholesale 
Prices of both large and small nuts              
have increased by 3% and 2% 
respectively. Price ranges were Rs.200.00- 
210.00 for a large nut and Rs.170.00 - 
180.00 for a small nut.  
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the current wholesale prices of large and 
small nuts have increased by 101% and 
109% respectively. 
 
       Retail 
Prices of large and small nuts showe d a 
price increment of Rs.5.00/nut and 
Rs.8.00/nut respectively. Price ranges were 
Rs.210.00-220.00/kg for a large nut and 
Rs. 180.00 -195.00/kg for a small nut. 
Compared to the same period last year, 
prices of both large nuts and small nuts 
have increased  by 91% and 97% 
respectively. 
 
Fruits 
Wholesale 
Prices of most of fruit varieties have 
decreased due to  high supply and low 
demand during the end of the festive 
season. The highest price decrease was 
recorded for passion fruit, at 38%. Prices of 
all banana varieties have decreased in the 
range of 2% -34% with prices of 
karthakolomban and slime apple have 
increased by 109% and 12% respectively.  
Wholesale prices for the most of fruits have 
decreased compared to last year, 60% 
decrease was recorded for large pineapple. 
 
    Retail 
In line with the decreased wholesale prices, 
retail prices of most of the fruit varieties 
have decreased. The highest price 
decreased of Rs.27.00/kg was reported for 
imported grapes. Price of banana varieties 
kolikuttu, ambul and seeni has decreased in 
the range of Rs.24.00 -3.00/kg. Further, 
Prices of all sizes of pineapple have 
decreased in the range of Rs.20.00 -
23.00/kg. Price of papaw has decreased by 
Rs. 17.00/kg. 
However, the price of karthakolomban 
mango has increased b y Rs.271.00/kg. 
Price of orange(local), slime                      
apple    and wood  apple have increased                    
by Rs.24.00/fruit,  Rs.23.00/fruit and 
Rs.12.00/fruit respectively.  
Compared to the same period of last year, 
current prices of most of the fruits have 
decreased and the highest price decrease of 
55% was reported for large size pineapples.  
 
Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin  
 
6  
  
 
Fresh Fish  
Wholesale 
Wholesale prices of most of the fresh fish 
types have decrea sed due to increased 
supply. The highest price decrease of 14% 
was noted for salaya due to high supply 
from Mannar, Negombo and Trincomalee 
areas. The prices decrease of 11% -12% 
were noted for shrimps, paraw and hurulla 
due to high supply from Kalpitiya, C hilaw 
and Northern areas. However, a price 
increase of 13% was noted for thora due to 
low supply from Beruwala and 
Valachchenai areas. Prices of other fresh 
fish varieties have not changed 
significantly.  
Compared to the same period of last year, 
current wholesale prices of all of the fresh 
fish types except shrimps have increased 
with the highest price increase of 47% was 
noted for balaya.  
 
 
Retail 
 The prices of other fish varieties except 
balaya, kelawalla and thora have decreased 
by 14% the highest price decrease was 
recorded for hurulla by Rs.288.00/kg. The 
second highest price decrease was recorded 
for shrimps by Rs.72.00/kg. The highest 
price increase was recorded for balaya by 
Rs.181.00/kg. Price increase of kelawalla 
and thora have r ecorded as Rs.124.00/kg 
and Rs.172.00/kg respectively. 
 
 Compared to the same period of last year, 
the prices of other fish varieties except 
thora and shrimps have increased and the 
highest price increase was recorded for 
balaya by 55%. 
 
     Dried Fish 
    Wholesale 
Prices of most of the dried fish types have 
increased due to decrease of local dried fish 
supply. A price increase of 19% was noted 
for local sprats due to low supply from 
Trincomalee, Mannar and Mullathivu 
areas. A price increase of 6% was noted for 
local balaya due to low supply from 
Negombo and Beruwala areas. However, a 
price decrease of 5% was noted for 
imported sprats due to high supply from 
Iran and Thailand. Prices of other dried fish 
verities have not changed significantly.

7  
  
 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
current wholesale prices of all of the dried 
fish types except imported sprats have 
increased and the highest price increase of 
103% was noted for local salaya.  
 
Retail 
The prices of other dry fish varieties except 
imported sprats have increased and the 
highest price increase was recorded for 
maduwa by Rs.100.00/kg. Price increase of 
mora and aguluwa have recorded as 
Rs.52.00/kg and Rs.40.00/kg respectively. 
Price decrease of imported sprats has 
recorded as Rs.13.00/kg. 
Compared to the same period of last year, 
the prices of other dry fish varieties except 
local and imported sprats have increased 
and the highest price increase was recorded 
for salaya by 23%. 
 
Eggs 
Wholesale 
The prices of both brown and white eggs 
have decreased by 41% and 43% 
respectively and their prices                             
ranged    Rs.23.00-27.00/egg and Rs.21.50-
24.00/egg respectively.  
Compared to the same period of last year, 
prices of both brow n and white eggs have 
decreased by 49% and 51% respectively.  
Retail 
 The prices of both brown and white egg 
types have decreased and the price range of 
a white egg has recorded as Rs.24.00 -
Rs.35.00 and the price range of a brown 
egg has recorded as Rs.25 .00-36.00. The 
average price of the white and brown egg 
types has recorded as Rs.27.00 and 
Rs.29.00 respectively.  
 Compared to the same period of last year, 
the prices of both egg varieties have 
decreased by 46%. 
 
Meat 
Retail 
Price decrease of broiler and curry chicken 
have recorded as Rs.77.00/kg and 
Rs.50.00/kg respectively. Price increase of 
mutton and pork have recorded as 
Rs.33.00/kg and Rs.150.00/kg 
respectively.  
 Compared to the same period of last year, 
other meat type s except broiler and curry 
chicken have increased and the highest 
price increase was recorded pork by 22%.  
Weekly Food Commodities Bulletin  
 
8  
  
 
 
 
 
EXPLANATORY NOTE  
The Food Commodities Bulletin is a weekly 
publication containing information relating 
to wholesale and retail prices of selected 
food commodities in nine selected markets 
in and around Colombo and main markets 
in the major producing areas.  
The information is analyzed and presented 
as prevailing prices, price ranges, averages, 
and a comparison of weekly prices. The 
changes in prices reported are always with 
price prevailed during the previous week 
unless otherwise stated.  
The frequency of collection of wholesale 
price information is twice a week ( i.e., 
Tuesday and Friday) while with retail 
prices, it is thrice a week ( i.e., Friday, 
Monday, and Tuesday).
